People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me today. Um, I hope this is the right kind of thing to come in for... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing. 

I work as an artist, and lately, I guess I've been getting some feedback at work that I'm... well, a little hard to follow? Like, when I'm putting together my artist statements, or sending notes to clients, or even just talking in our meetings. I guess my words kind of jump around a little bit. I'll be explaining the physical textures of a piece, and then suddenly I'm talking about the budget, and then... um, well, I've been seeing these sort of deeper, secret meanings in things lately, and I try to explain how it all connects. 

It all makes perfect sense in my head, I promise, but people keep telling me they just can’t understand what I’m trying to explain. It's a little embarrassing, honestly, because I really want to do a good job. It's probably just a little stress making me scatterbrained, right? Is that... does that make sense as a reason to be here?

I guess I started talking about the textures I wanted to use, and then how the budget tied into the textures, and... then I think I started explaining the secret meanings I'd found in the gallery's emails about the space. \n\nI thought I was making perfect sense in the moment, but... they just looked so confused. They told me they couldn't follow what I was trying to explain at all. It's actually kind of why I came in... my artist statements and client notes have been doing the same thing, just jumping all over the place. \n\nIt made me feel really awful, honestly. I don't want people to think I'm unprofessional or difficult to work with. I always try to be so clear! Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too scattered right now. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: How long do these feelings of secret meanings and hearing the voice typically last when they happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been going on for a little while now. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I want to be professional and clear, especially when I'm working. But... like I mentioned before about why I came in... it's just getting so hard to control. 

When I'm trying to explain something, like in that client meeting, or even when I'm writing my artist statements or my notes... I'll start out okay, but then I'm suddenly talking about the textures, and then how much the budget is, and then the secret meanings I'm seeing... and I don't even realize I've drifted off until people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying at all. 

I really want to be able to just stay on one topic, and I'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now with you. Am I making sense? I just... I try to refocus, but it's like my brain just keeps jumping around before I can catch it. Is that... is that normal for what I'm going through?

I don't let it ruin my productivity or bother anyone else in the building. Am I... am I explaining that okay for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of details.\n\nDoctor: Do the messages from the voice and the secret meanings you see in things seem to be related to each other in some way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, I haven't really noticed a connection between them. The voice is usually just making little comments, and the meanings in the stains or the emails feel like... like separate instructions, if that makes sense. \n\nI hope it's okay that they aren't related. I know sometimes these things are supposed to fit together like a puzzle, but for me, they just kind of happen on their own. Is it... is it bad if they aren't connected? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information. I promise I'm trying really hard to pay attention to all of it so I can answer your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Are these experiences, like hearing the voice or seeing secret meanings, causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes, I guess I have. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, it's not like I'm completely out of control or anything! I just... for stretches, I get this feeling where I'm just lit up. Like I'm almost unstoppable, and every single idea I have for my art is just brilliant. It's actually a really productive feeling, usually. I get so much done.

But... well, sometimes it gets a little intense. Like, if I'm working with a collaborator and they make just a small comment or suggestion, I might... snap at them. And I know it's an overreaction, it feels way bigger than the situation actually warrants. I feel terrible afterward, I really do. I don't want to be difficult to work with, I promise. Do you think that's... is that part of what's wrong with me? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Like I'm almost unstoppable, and every single idea I have for my art is just brilliant. It's actually a really productive feeling, usually. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, those stretches where I'm just so energized and, you know, maybe a little irritable with people... and where I'm barely sleeping because I'm juggling way too many art projects... they've kind of been happening for a while now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes, I definitely think my art is better during those times. I hope it doesn't sound arrogant to say that! I just... I get this feeling that what I'm doing is really, really important. 

Like, when I'm at my most charged up, I honestly feel like I'm about to completely change how everyone understands installation art. It just feels so clear to me, you know? And I start thinking that normal things that hold people back—like getting funding, or not having enough time, or even just my own level of experience—none of that really applies to me in those moments. I just feel like I can transcend all of it. 

I know that probably sounds a little grandiose, and I really hope you don't think I'm full of myself! It's just this incredible surge of confidence. Is it okay to feel that strongly about my work? I mean, a lot of successful artists have to believe in themselves that much, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right impression... I'm really just very passionate.

Like, when I'm putting together my artist statements, or sending notes to clients, or even just talking in our meetings. I guess my words kind of jump around a little bit. I'll be explaining the physical textures of a piece, and then suddenly I'm talking about the budget, and then... um, well, I've been seeing these sort of deeper, secret meanings in things lately, and I try to explain how it all connects. \n\nIt all makes perfect sense in my head, I promise, but people keep telling me they just can’t understand what I’m trying to explain. It's a little embarrassing, honestly, because I really want to do a good job. It's probably just a little stress making me scatterbrained, right? Is that... does that make sense as a reason to be here?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'secret meanings' that you've been seeing in things lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's... I know it sounds a little silly when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm crazy or anything. It's just... sometimes, I'll be looking at things, like a weird stain on the sidewalk outside the studio, or the way a seam is sewn on a canvas, or even just a specific phrase in an email from a gallery... and I get this really strong feeling that it's meant specifically for me. \n\nLike, it's a little instruction or a warning about my art. And, um, it feels so real in the moment. Even when my friends or coworkers try to point out that it's just a coffee stain or a typo, it's... it's really hard for me to let go of that feeling. I try to listen to them, I really do, but it just feels so important. Is that... is that a normal thing to experience when you're working really hard on a project? I'm sure it's just my imagination getting a little carried away, right?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these secret meanings in things?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe roughly 11 months? I hope that doesn't sound too strange... that I've been noticing it for that long, I mean. That's about when I first started seeing the, um, the messages in my art materials. \n\nAnd... well... I haven't really mentioned this next part to my coworkers because I really don't want them to think I'm doing a bad job, but... around that same time, I also started noticing these weird smells in the studio. And sometimes... sometimes I'll even hear a voice in there, too, when no one else is around. \n\nI know that probably sounds really bad, I'm sorry. I swear I try my best to just ignore it and not let it bother anyone! It's probably just my brain working overtime from being cooped up alone in the studio so much, right? Is... is this the kind of information you need me to share? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: When you're hearing the voice in the studio, does it sound like it's speaking in full sentences or more like fragmented words?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's more like full sentences? I really hope that doesn't make me sound too crazy. It's just... it sounds like a woman, and she's just quietly commenting on things, usually from right behind the storage shelves. \n\nAnd, um, I also get this really strong smell of hot metal sometimes, right near an unfinished piece I'm working on. In the moment, the voice and the smell, they feel completely real to me. Like, totally normal. But then I'll stop and go to check who's behind the shelves, or look for where the burning smell is coming from, and... there's just nothing there. \n\nI promise I try to just shake it off and keep working when it happens! I don't let it ruin my productivity or bother anyone else in the building. Am I... am I explaining that okay for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of details.\n\nDoctor: Do the messages from the voice and the secret meanings you see in things seem to be related to each other in some way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, I haven't really noticed a connection between them. The voice is usually just making little comments, and the meanings in the stains or the emails feel like... like separate instructions, if that makes sense. \n\nI hope it's okay that they aren't related. I know sometimes these things are supposed to fit together like a puzzle, but for me, they just kind of happen on their own. Is it... is it bad if they aren't connected? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information. I promise I'm trying really hard to pay attention to all of it so I can answer your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Are these experiences, like hearing the voice or seeing secret meanings, causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess maybe they have been interfering a little bit? I mean, I really try my hardest not to let it affect my work, I promise. I always want to do a good job. But... I guess I have missed a couple of paid deadlines recently. I just... I get so distracted by trying to figure out the meanings in things. \n\nAnd, um, I haven't wanted to tell anyone this because I feel so embarrassed, but... I think I've been confusing my collaborators in meetings lately. And... well, my gallery actually had to postpone an exhibition because... they said my notes and proposals just weren't making enough sense to use anymore. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I really am trying to keep everything together, I swear! I just... I want to be a good artist and a good collaborator. Is... is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture, even if it's a little embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a recent conversation where you felt like you were having trouble getting your point across, and how that made you feel?\n\nPatient: Um... well, there was a meeting last week with a potential client. I really wanted it to go well, I promise I prepared for it! But... um, they asked about my timeline for the piece, and I tried to explain it, but... I guess I started talking about the textures I wanted to use, and then how the budget tied into the textures, and... then I think I started explaining the secret meanings I'd found in the gallery's emails about the space. \n\nI thought I was making perfect sense in the moment, but... they just looked so confused. They told me they couldn't follow what I was trying to explain at all. It's actually kind of why I came in... my artist statements and client notes have been doing the same thing, just jumping all over the place. \n\nIt made me feel really awful, honestly. I don't want people to think I'm unprofessional or difficult to work with. I always try to be so clear! Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too scattered right now. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: How long do these feelings of secret meanings and hearing the voice typically last when they happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been going on for a little while now. I mean, I hope it's not too long, I don't want you to think I've just been ignoring it or anything! I've really been trying to handle it on my own. But... I guess the messages I see in the materials, and the voice... and sometimes there are these smells in the studio, too... they've been happening for, um, roughly 11 months? \n\nI know that probably sounds like a long time to wait before getting help, and I'm really sorry if I should have come in sooner. I just kept thinking I could manage it, you know? Like maybe it was just part of my creative process or something. Is... is 11 months really bad? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact timeframe so we can fix it. I promise I'm ready to do whatever I need to do now.\n\nDoctor: Do the messages from the voice and the secret meanings you see in things ever conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I hope that's the right answer! I haven't really noticed them disagreeing with each other or anything like that. It's more like... they just kind of happen? \n\nI really try to just focus on my work and not let them confuse me too much, I promise. They don't seem to fight with each other or tell me opposite things... at least, I don't think they do? Is that... is it okay that they don't conflict? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I just haven't really noticed them clashing.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start jumping between different topics, do you feel like you're able to stop and refocus on one thing if you try?\n\nPatient: Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I want to be professional and clear, especially when I'm working. But... like I mentioned before about why I came in... it's just getting so hard to control. \n\nWhen I'm trying to explain something, like in that client meeting, or even when I'm writing my artist statements or my notes... I'll start out okay, but then I'm suddenly talking about the textures, and then how much the budget is, and then the secret meanings I'm seeing... and I don't even realize I've drifted off until people tell me they can't follow what I'm saying at all. \n\nI really want to be able to just stay on one topic, and I'm so sorry if I'm doing it right now with you. Am I making sense? I just... I try to refocus, but it's like my brain just keeps jumping around before I can catch it. Is that... is that normal for what I'm going through?\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start jumping between different topics, do you feel like other people can usually understand what you're trying to communicate?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just saying a second ago—and I'm so sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions exactly right!—but... no, they really can't. \n\nThat's actually a big part of why I finally came in. People keep telling me they just can't follow what I'm trying to explain at all. I'll be writing my artist statements, or my client notes, or talking in those meetings, and I guess I just start jumping from the textures, to the budgets, to the secret meanings... and they just get totally lost. \n\nI feel so bad when it happens, because I really, really want to be clear and professional. I hope you don't think I'm just being careless with my work! I promise I'm trying my best to be understood. Am I... am I making sense to you right now? I just want to make sure I'm communicating this okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these secret meanings and voices all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... they don't always happen exactly the same way, if that makes sense? I hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nLike... there was this one month where things were a lot quieter. I was actually sleeping more normally, and I didn't have that feeling of being totally unstoppable and brilliant all the time. I was just trying to be really good and focus on my art. But... even then, when I was doing everything right, I still kept hearing those little comments coming from behind the studio shelves. And I was still seeing those hidden warnings in my normal art supplies for, like, several weeks during that time. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound completely crazy... I know it sounds strange that it kept happening even when I was sleeping and feeling calmer. Is it okay that they didn't go away completely? I just want to be honest with you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any periods of abnormally elevated or depressed mood over the past 11 months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I guess I have. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, it's not like I'm completely out of control or anything! I just... for stretches, I get this feeling where I'm just lit up. Like I'm almost unstoppable, and every single idea I have for my art is just brilliant. It's actually a really productive feeling, usually. I get so much done.\n\nBut... well, sometimes it gets a little intense. Like, if I'm working with a collaborator and they make just a small comment or suggestion, I might... snap at them. And I know it's an overreaction, it feels way bigger than the situation actually warrants. I feel terrible afterward, I really do. I don't want to be difficult to work with, I promise. Do you think that's... is that part of what's wrong with me? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling persistently sad or depressed over the past 11 months?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I hope that's the right answer, but I haven't really felt sad or depressed like that. \n\nI know some artists get really down and, you know, have those dark periods, but I don't really get like that. Even when things are a little overwhelming, I try really hard to stay positive and keep working. I don't just sit around feeling sad for days on end or anything. I really try to be a happy, functional person, you know? \n\nIs it... is it okay that I don't have that? I know I have the really high, brilliant moods, but the sad ones just aren't really a thing for me. I hope I'm not leaving anything important out!\n\nDoctor: When you have those feelings of being lit up and unstoppable, how long do they typically last?\n\nPatient: Um... well, those stretches where I'm just so energized and, you know, maybe a little irritable with people... and where I'm barely sleeping because I'm juggling way too many art projects... they've kind of been happening for a while now. \n\nI'd say it's been a part of my life for about 10 months? I hope that doesn't sound too bad. I mean, it's not like every single day is completely chaotic, I swear! I still get a lot of good work done during those times. Is 10 months a really long time to be dealing with that? I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling lit up and unstoppable, do you find that you're taking on more projects or responsibilities than you can realistically handle?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do take on a bit more than usual, but it's all good things! I hope it doesn't sound like I'm being reckless or anything. It's just that I get so inspired, you know? \n\nLike, I'll suddenly agree to do three different exhibition proposals at once, and then I'll decide I need to completely reorganize my entire studio space right then and there. And I'll be sketching all through the night, sending out emails to curators, and even starting to cut materials for new pieces before I've even figured out the budget for them. \n\nI know it sounds like a lot, but I just get so passionate about the work. I really try to stay on top of it all, I promise. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm totally disorganized, I just get really excited about my art... is that a bad thing?\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling lit up and unstoppable, do you think your art is better or more creative than when you're in a normal mood?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I definitely think my art is better during those times. I hope it doesn't sound arrogant to say that! I just... I get this feeling that what I'm doing is really, really important. \n\nLike, when I'm at my most charged up, I honestly feel like I'm about to completely change how everyone understands installation art. It just feels so clear to me, you know? And I start thinking that normal things that hold people back—like getting funding, or not having enough time, or even just my own level of experience—none of that really applies to me in those moments. I just feel like I can transcend all of it. \n\nI know that probably sounds a little grandiose, and I really hope you don't think I'm full of myself! It's just this incredible surge of confidence. Is it okay to feel that strongly about my work? I mean, a lot of successful artists have to believe in themselves that much, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right impression... I'm really just very passionate.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling lit up and unstoppable, do you ever find yourself engaging in risky behaviors that you might not normally do when you're in a normal mood?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm out there doing crazy things or anything! I try to be really responsible, even when I'm super energized. \n\nI guess... well, I might spend a little more money on art supplies than I planned, but it's always for a good reason, you know? Like, I just need the right materials for the vision I have. And maybe I'll stay out a bit later talking to people at gallery openings, but that's just networking, right? It's important for my career. \n\nI really don't do anything dangerous or, like, reckless. I wouldn't want to mess up my life or my art. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping things under control, even when I feel really unstoppable. That's a good thing, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um... well, yes, I suppose I do have times like that. Usually they last a week, sometimes a bit longer. Mostly it's just a really wonderful feeling! I just kept thinking I could manage it, you know? Like maybe it was just part of my creative process or something. Is... is 11 months really bad? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Am I making sense? I just... I try to refocus, but it's like my brain just keeps jumping around before I can catch it. People keep telling me they just can't follow what I'm trying to explain at all.
